# HomeCraft Management — Plugin Design Specification (v17.27)
> **Purpose of this document:** the build spec for a custom Paper plugin. It is written to be handed to Claude Code (or any implementer) as the source of truth. Design decisions still open are marked **[DECISION]** with a recommended default.
>
> **v11 changelog:** Rebranded the online store from "Amazon" to **Crate** (`[www.Crate.craft](https://www.Crate.craft)`), with **Rush** (fast shipping), the **Pallet** (player seller box) and the **Locker** (delivery holding). Added the **Crate Marketplace** (universal player-to-player selling — *everything* is sellable, incl. Minis), **auto-categorization into departments** using the game's own item categories, an **admin ban list**, and the **PC-as-a-browser / "Sites"** architecture. Added **in-game economy displays** (TVs/tickers/boards) and a consolidated **Economy Risks & Safeguards** section. Recorded the **Phase 2.5.1 pricing fix** (proportional elasticity + integrated bulk pricing). Marked Phases 2.5 and 3 done.
>
> **v17.27 changelog (0.38.4-golem-and-clubhouse — the Copper Golem Brush, invites you can tap, challenges, the record firework, splits, the trophy case, the jukebox corner, the family week):** The owner's decisions of 29 Oct: for the golem "No, let's make this a special tool - take something like the brush and make it a different colour and name it something different. If we can make it copper that would be great. To craft it: lots of copper plus feathers and maybe a slime ball", then the recipe "Feather / Feather / Feather; Blank / Slime Ball / Blank; Blank / Copper Block (any) / Blank"; and the Clubhouse extras in one release. No config revision bump (24: the `golem` section is backfilled); **schema v39** (`game_splits`). **The Copper Golem Brush (`golem/`).** A tagged, glinting, UNCOMMON, unbreakable `BRUSH` (`GolemBrush`, key `homecraftmanagement:golem_brush`; `/hcm give golem_brush`), never a token prize; a `ShapedRecipe` FFF / " S " / " C " with C a `MaterialChoice` over the eight copper blocks, from `golem.recipe` (`GolemConfig`, per-symbol fallbacks with one WARN each), registered on enable and removed on disable; the vanilla brush texture cannot be tinted without a resource pack (a later option through CraftBridge's pack). The filter is Paper's `ItemTransportingEntityValidateTargetEvent` (not `Cancellable`: `setAllowed(false)` only, never `ignoreCancelled`), whose deny means "not a container for this search, re-asked next time" (Paper's `TransportItemsBetweenContainers` patch ANDs it into `isValidTarget`): for a golem with an assignment every `Tag.COPPER_CHESTS` block (the source) and every assigned position is allowed and every other container denied, in a pure `GolemRules.allow(isCopperChest, assigned, packed)`; the handler is one map get, one tag test and one set lookup, no allocation, no log. The store is the golem's `PersistentDataContainer` (a `LONG_ARRAY` of 26/12/26-bit packed x/y/z, the world key, the base name), read into `Map<UUID, Set<Long>>` on start, `EntitiesLoadEvent` and `EntityAddToWorldEvent`, dropped on remove and death, a position whose block is no longer a container pruned lazily by a 20-tick pass that also keeps the name tag ("Rusty -> 3 chests" / "Rusty - holding Cobblestone"; a name the golem was given is kept; `GolemRules.tag/base/isTag`). Gestures: a plain or shift tap on a golem with the brush in the main hand opens a 60 s "pick its chests" session (`GolemRules.Sessions`: OPEN/CLOSE/SWITCH/ARM_CLEAR/CLEAR, a 2 s clear window, `Taps` 600 ms); a chest, trapped chest or barrel tapped in a session is linked or unlinked at LOWEST with `setUseInteractedBlock(DENY)` + cancel (CraftBridge's LOW `ignoreCancelled` handler skips), both halves of a double chest, at most 32, and the block PDC byte `craftbridge:golem_chest = 1` is set by its string key (never a compile dependency) and never removed by an unlink; per-viewer "#N" `TextDisplay` markers (`GolemMarkers`, RemoteMarkers' pattern, tag `GOLEM_MARKER`, swept on load); putting the brush away, a swap, a quit or a world change ends the session. Outside a session the brush passes a chest through, never brushes suspicious sand or gravel, and does nothing in a game session. Reach is vanilla's: a 65x17x65 box around the golem and 1 up / 2 down (the docs say so). Left for the owner's live test: the idle look while every drop target is denied, whether a Bedrock shift-tap on an entity arrives as a sneak (a plain tap opens too, so the daughter never needs it), the event's real rate. **The Play? screen (`games/Invites`, `gui/games/InviteAskMenu`).** Every invite opens a 27-slot screen on the invitee (Not now 11, the asker 13 with the seconds ticking, Yes 15, as `TradeAskMenu`): `GamesScreens.askInvite/closeAsk` defaults, `Screens` implements them, `InviteAskMenu extends GameMenu implements Invites.AskScreen`; the pure `Invites.askScreenOpens(noContainer, cursorEmpty, inSession, hangingOut)`; `screenTries` (1 until shown, `SHOWN` after: an Esc is not an answer and it never re-opens) with one `host.later(20)` retry chain; `closeAsk` from `expire`, `cancel`, `cancelFrom`, `cancelGame`, `take` and `clear`; Java keeps the chat buttons, and `TradeService.offer` tries the Trade? screen for Java too. **Challenge anyone (`PartyRaces.challenge/challengeOn/sendChallenge`, `ChallengeMenu`, `ChallengePickerMenu`, `CourseMenu.CHALLENGE_SLOT = 19`, the kit's `CHALLENGE` at slot 1 while not in a party, sig `(party ? "P" : "C")`).** A challenge is a 1-vs-1 race party (`Parties.create/send/answered`): the picker lists everyone online, green tappable, grey with the reason in the name (pure `challengeNote`: in a game, in a party, invites off, busy; nobody mid-run is reached), the invite is the `trials` key named "Race challenge", and the pure `yesToClubhouse` sends the invitee's Yes into the Clubhouse as a party member (`Clubhouse.go`), the host's Start seating both from there; with the Clubhouse off, the ordinary lobby. **NEW RECORD (`ClubDoor.soloResult(p, opener, recordLine)` default, `Clubhouse.Mine.recordLine`, `Clubhouse.newRecord`, `ClubSolo.recordLine`, `ClubGolf.recordLine`, golf's record flag carried out of `GolfRounds.finishLines/record`).** A solo run or round alone that set the all-time record and lands in the Clubhouse: the title for everyone in the room, `BoardDisplay.firework` over podium 1, the chat line; outside the Clubhouse `Fireworks.spawn` at the player; a round changed while played is never a record. **Splits (`games/trial/Splits`, pure; `game_splits(player, course, layout, times, at)` schema v39; `GamesDao.splits/saveSplits`, `SPLITS_MAX` 128; `TrialRun.bestSplits`; `TimeTrials.loadSplits/settledSplits`).** On a counted solo personal best the run's checkpoint times are stored per course and layout hash; later runs' checkpoint action bar adds `deltaText` ("+0.4 s" red / "-0.6 s" green, tenths floored) and the finish `finishLine` says from which checkpoint the gap kept its sign; never in a race or a warm-up (a race's run is on the raced course, whose targets differ); `AccountMoveDao.NOT_MOVED` lists the table. **The trophy case (`games/clubhouse/TrophyWall`, `ClubhousePlanner.trophyFrames`, `ClubhouseRoom.trophies`).** One fixed, no-drop `ItemFrame` (gold ingot for a course, golf ball for golf) with a FIXED `TextDisplay` line per all-time record on the Fame list, at most `ClubBoard.MAX_ROWS`, on the west wall's skirting row under My stats, tagged and swept via `WorldEntities`, redrawn from `ClubReads`' cache in `ClubBoards.second` only where a row changed; owner-built rooms `/hcm games clubhouse trophies`. **The jukebox corner (`ClubhousePlanner` ALGO 2, hash re-pinned `948a67d26f10`; `Songs`, `JukeboxCorner`, `gui/games/clubhouse/SongMenu`).** A pink glass stage with `minecraft:jukebox[has_record=false]` in `COVERED_ONLY` and a glass cap (spawn-proof) in the south-west corner; existing rooms converge at the next start; a LOWEST `PlayerInteractEvent` on that block denies and cancels (vanilla never pops the disc, the Arcade Machine listener never sees it) and opens the `SongMenu` (one disc tile per `JukeboxSong`, titles from the keys, Quiet); a tap plays for the room ("<name> picked Pigstep"), a second queues ("Next: ..."), `Songs.Queue` is pure; stop = `stopPlaying` + inventory `clear()`, never `eject()`, on stopRoom, the closed branch, a move, disable, and a crash-left disc is swept when the room opens; the Together tab's Songs tile (`club_songs`) while inside; owner-built rooms `/hcm games clubhouse jukebox`. **The family week (`FamilyWeekRules` pure, `FamilyWeek`, `GamesDao.addPointsCapped`).** RUN 1, PERSONAL_BEST 2, WEEK_BEST 3, RECORD 5, PARTY_WIN 3, GOLF_ROUND 1, HOLE_IN_ONE 3, CHEER 1, `DAY_CAP` 10, on boards `club:<weekKey>` / `clubday:<dayKey>` / `clubwins:<weekKey>` of game `clubhouse` written in one transaction; the one hook `FamilyWeek.scored` at `TimeTrials.progress`, `GolfRounds.progress`, `PartyRaces.finish` (`partyWon`) and `Clubhouse.cheer` (a heard cheer), each a once-per-event place; the week is the wall boards' local-midnight week on `arcade.quests.week_starts` (`DailyLookup.weekStart`), NOT the Cup's 04:00 week; `FameBoard`'s fourth page "Family week", `StatsCard` "This week: 9 pts, 2nd" (`WALL_BESTS` 4 -> 3 keeps the wall card at 9 lines), `MyStatsMenu` slot 20; the ceremony once a second on a cache that carries this week's key: `FamilyWeekRules.turn` (the marker `gen.clubhouse.week.done` written BEFORE `club.podium`, expires after 7 days, respects `closingForRestart` and `photoPending`), two of last week's top three in the room, "Photo time!" with "Last week's top three", the firework over 1st, `lastLine`. **Tests:** `GolemRulesTest`, `GolemConfigTest`, `GolemTextTest`; `InvitesTest`, `InviteAskMenuLayoutTest`, `CourseMenuLayoutTest`, `PartyRaceTest`, `ClubhouseRacesTest`, `ClubSoloTest`, `ClubGolfTest`, `SplitsTest`, `GamesDaoSplitsTest`, `DatabaseMigrationTest` (v39), `AccountMoveTest`; `ClubhousePlannerTest`, `ClubhouseRoomTest`, `TrophyWallTest`, `SongsTest`, `FamilyWeekRulesTest`, `GamesDaoPointsCapTest`, `ClubDataTest`, `FameBoardTest`, `StatsCardTest`, `ClubBoardTextTest`, `WallBoardsTest`, `ClubhouseCopyTest`. **Back to 0.38.3:** the jar alone; the `golem` section is read by nothing and ignored silently (no top-level unknown-key check in 0.38.3) and one WARN for schema v39; the corner converges away (ALGO 1); points rows, hand spots and the week marker stay unread; golems keep their chests in their own data, unread; nothing is lost.
>
> **v17.26 changelog (0.38.3-clubhouse — through the Clubhouse, its boards, the Arcade by rows):** The owner's requests of 26 Oct: "Can you make it so we go to the clubhouse when click into a game? I just feel like the clubhouse isnt really used. Also a quick reorganization of the Arcade now that we have added all the stuff its a bit out of place."; then, for the room: "For the Clubhouse we should display the top runs of the week and such... Maybe some stats or something? Have to make it a reason to be there after all", and "the board I have now doesn't work great as it rotates as you walk so it only works if you are directly in front of it. Is there a way to lock it in place?". The answers: courses (every kind) and mini golf go through the Clubhouse; cabinets and luck games open where you stand, and cabinets also from a Games item inside; luck games, crates, the Scratch Ticket and card packs stay outside; three boards of four offered (not the live course map). **Through the Clubhouse (design C1)**, behind `games.clubhouse.solo_first` / `solo_after` / `first_seconds` (1-30, shipped 5) and `boards`, all shipped true (no revision bump: 24; schema v38; the backfill adds the keys; `ClubhouseSettings` keeps 0.38.2's 6-argument constructor); `solo_first: false` is design C2, and with the Clubhouse off every path is 0.38.2's. New pure classes: `ClubRoute` (HAND_OUT / LOBBY / DIRECT, `after`, `leaveBack`: every fallback in one place: the door null, another world, the restart hold, a rider booked, a test run, another session), `ClubLobby` (the countdown, Go now, Stay here; a pending trip is busy for `ClubVisits.second`), `ClubNext` (the trip, carrying the warm-up choice in `Clubhouse.Arrival`); new `games/trial/ClubSolo` (start through, hand-out from, back to the Clubhouse; modelled on `ClubRaces`). Routing lives in `TimeTrials.begin` / `ready` + `readyFromClub` + `arm` / `finish` / `showResult` and `GolfRounds.start` / `finish` / `leftToClub`, `ClubGolf.lobby` / `soloBack` / `soloTake` / `leftToClub` (`fromClubhouse` now for a round alone), so every start path (screens, Today's pick, join signs, `/hcm play <course>`, Play again) behaves the same; `ResultMenu` and `GolfScorecardMenu` light Play again in the Clubhouse (slot 22 / 49 "Back to the courses" there). `ClubDoor` gains `soloFirst`, `soloAfter`, `soloResult` (defaults: the fakes stay 0.38.2 doors). Solo results are per player (`Clubhouse.mine`), never `last` / the board. Play gate step 0: a Clubhouse visitor may open trials, Fresh Courses, the Cup, golf and cabinets; `Refusal.NOT_IN_CLUBHOUSE` ("That one isn't played in the Clubhouse - tap Leave game first.") for the rest and the Games screen's links. `Game.onLeaveItem` + `WorldSessions.leaveItem`: the kit guard's Leave game asks the game first (partway through a run started from the Clubhouse it goes back there); `/hcm leave` never does. The Clubhouse's tile on Together while open; kit slots 0 Party, 2 Results, 3 Go now, 4 Watch live, 5 Stay here, 6 Games, 7 My stats, 8 Leave game; a cabinet screen counts as busy; Connect Four / Tic-Tac-Toe invites reach a visitor hanging out (`Clubhouse.hangingOut`). **The boards.** The results board and three new wall boards are FIXED text displays, placed by the pure `WallBoards`: north/south, east/west, 0.06 off the wall's face, bottom 7.25 above the floor block, scale 2; the results board wraps at 200 px to fit its panel, the walls at 280 px; each padded to 9 lines; `BoardDisplay` remakes any display that is not FIXED, has turned or has moved (old VERTICAL boards are replaced on start). An owner-built board takes the admin's yaw, snapped to N/E/S/W, at 1.5x; `/hcm games clubhouse board fame|playing|stats` sets the walls' boards (`gen.clubhouse.hand.board.<which>`). **This week's best** (`FameBoard`, pages every 8 s, the weekly boards' own week boundaries, an All-time records page with Fresh courses' set records), **Now playing** (`NowBoard`, from the read-only `TrialsNow` / `GolfNow`, once a second, up to 7 rows, the cheer line) and **My stats** (`StatsCard`: bests with `GamesDao.rank`, runs this week, best golf round, tokens from the ledger's `GAMES_*` sources less the Cup's entry and refund; tokens never become money) are drawn by `ClubBoards`. My stats is a per-viewer display in `ViewerBoards` (hidden by default, `showEntity`, as `RemoteMarkers`; cleaned up on leave, quit and disable), plus the kit's slot 7 (PAPER) and a Bedrock-safe `MyStatsMenu` screen. The first database reads off the main thread: `ClubReads` (a snapshot on main, `ClubData.read` on the scheduler's async pool over the DAOs, the result applied on main; one read in flight, every 30 s while anyone is in, 5 s after an arrival, late results dropped by generation, a failed read keeps the cache and warns once a minute; the DAOs already lock the one SQLite connection per statement). New `GamesDao.boardsOf` and a public `GamesDao.rank` (0 with no score). No block of the room changes (plan hash unchanged); every entity tagged and swept as the results board; a failing board never switches the Clubhouse off (`ClubBoards` catches and warns once a minute). Bedrock: Geyser carries a text display over as an invisible armor stand's name tag and translates neither the billboard nor the scale, so there the boards still turn to face the viewer. **The Arcade by rows (A2).** `ArcadeMenu`'s rows by purpose: the wild-Mini tracker to row 0 (slot 8); **Play** on top (`PLAY` 9-17: All games, Today's pick, Courses, Mini golf, Cabinets, Together while Race Night or Falling Floors is open, The Clubhouse - N here while `Clubhouse.offered`, counting `Clubhouse.inside`, a click in through `games.open` like `/hcm play clubhouse`, High scores); the Prize Counter's tabs unchanged (19-26); **Luck** (crates 28-31, the ticket 32, Card Packs 33 (from 35), Games of luck 34 replacing the Play row's duplicate Luck door, Take a break 35, now with the games off too); **You** (Quests, Achievements, My Looks through `WalletMenu.myLooksTile` (now shared), How It Works, Give a present while `GiftPickerMenu.entryShown`). The Courses door's bug: it lit for any open TRIAL-kind game, so the Clubhouse, Race Night, Falling Floors or the Cup on their own opened an empty Courses tab; it lights now only for `courseTile` (TRIAL, open, `playables() > 0`). The hub's border pane is made in `build()`, so the class loads with no server and its constants are tested directly. **Prize Counter shelves** (`PrizeShelves`, pure): each kind from its own row while the tab fits a page (Boosts: boosts and the Quest Reroll, then the two COMMAND world-changers; COSMETICS: FIREWORK/BLOCK/REMOTE/PARTY, then TRAIL; LOOKS: HAT, then BALL; any other type a last row; every other tab one kind), else packed and paged as 0.38.2. `PrizeTab.COSMETICS`'s label is "Fireworks & Trails" (its name, the `tab:` key, the icon key `cosmetics` and the feed's `category: cosmetics` stay). No config, row, tab, price, revision or schema change. Tests: `ClubRouteTest`, `ClubLobbyTest`, `ClubSoloTest`, `PlayGateTest` (4 new), `ClubGolfTest` (5 new), `ClubhouseCopyTest` (ClubSolo scanned, the new kit names), `GamesMenuTest`; `WallBoardsTest`, `FameBoardTest`, `NowBoardTest`, `StatsCardTest`, `ClubBoardTextTest`, `ClubDataTest`, `ClubReadsTest`, `GamesDaoClubStatsTest`, `MyStatsMenuTest`, `ClubhouseRoomTest` (wall boards saved and read back, tab completion), `ClubhouseSettingsTest`, `EventsShippedDefaultsTest`; `PrizeCounterLayoutTest` (the shipped shelves on 26 Oct 2026 and in December, read from the bundled config; the packed fallback; every bundled row switched on still one page a tab), `PrizeTabSlotsTest` (`theHubsTabsAndPacks`, `theHubsPlayRowIsOnTop`, `theCoursesDoorLightsOnlyForACourseTile`, the label). **After review (R383):** the 3-2-1 after a hand-out holds while the run's own (async) move to the start is in flight (`TimeTrials.hold`: NONE / WAIT / AGAIN after `WAIT_TICKS`, armed by `readyFromClub`), so the whole countdown is had at the line; a solo result opens only once the player is seen in the room (`Clubhouse.soloOpen`, `RESULT_TRIES`), never before an async landing closes it; `/hcm play cheer` with no race going reaches everyone on a solo run or a round alone (`Cheers.solo`, "Nobody's in a game to cheer for right now." otherwise), as Now playing's cheer line promises; the kit's Results is named "Results - your last run" while the player's own result is the newer (`Clubhouse.ownNewer`, the kit re-given as it changes), and a run's or round's Leave game lore says it goes back to the Clubhouse when it does (`ClubhouseText.leaveLore`); the boards' week is `DailyLookup.weekStart` whether or not Time Trials is on (`TimeTrials.weekKey` delegates to it); `ClubReads` keeps who a read in flight is for, so a viewer who leaves meanwhile is not cached back; the hub's Mini golf door lights for a Golf-tab tile (`ArcadeMenu.golfTile`: Mini golf or Fresh Courses); `ClubRoute`'s javadoc says the hold applies to the start and closing-for-the-restart to the end (early in the hold a solo finish comes back and is sent home a minute later, as a race's); the games guide's course and golf walkthroughs and the README's collector passages describe the Clubhouse flow and the "Fireworks & Trails" shelves.
>
> **v17.25 changelog (0.38.2-firework-remote — the Firework Remote, the Super, Ultra and Ultimate shows, the Show Map and holiday shows):** The owner's requests of 25 Oct: "Let's make it so we can chain together up to 5 launchers! Then let's make it so we get a launcher tool that we can assign the launchers that we want to use in the show. That way I can build launchers around the city, leave them there, and when I buy the 5 launcher show or the 4 launcher show or the 3 launcher show I can then simply tag/click on the launchers I want to use.", "Or really as many launchers as you feel like you could do!", "Each player can have up to 10 launchers." (read as the biggest show's size; placed at once is 15, which the owner confirmed), "Let's make sure we have a way to tag it so I can see what launcher is what on a map or in real life by displaying it. Either on the tool or as a hologram.", "Is there any way we can see a map that would show them as well?" and "We can make this part of the holiday stuff too so we can make special holiday firework shows." **Config revision 24** (`FireworkRemoteMigration`, its own frozen literals; revision 23, which has already run on the owner's server, is untouched); no schema change (v38). **The tiers:** `firework_super` ("&bSuper Firework Show", 4 launchers, 100 tokens, 60 s), `firework_ultra` ("&6Ultra Firework Show", 5, 125, 60 s) and `firework_ultimate` ("&dUltimate Firework Show", 10, 200, 90 s) ship after `firework_mega` (3, 75, 60 s, unchanged), then `firework_remote`; all COSMETICS, no limit, not in the Arcade Crate; 100+ gets the confirm screen. `MegaShow.MAX_LAUNCHERS` is 10, so `PrizeExtra.launchers` reads 0-10 (one WARN above). **One plan for 3-10 lanes** (`MegaShow.plan(lanes, seconds)`, lanes = `max(3, launchers)`): the template moved from beats to 60-second ticks (`Cue`) and addresses zones (left, middle and right thirds, `round(N/3)` wide on the outside: 1/1/1, 1/2/1, 2/1/2 ... 3/4/3) and sweeps (lane j of N at `t0 + round(j × D / (N − 1))`, never two in a tick); with three lanes it is 0.38.1's 81 shots to the tick, order and fold at every length (`MegaShowLanesTest` holds a frozen copy of 0.38.1's cues). Every lane fires 27 rockets a minute (81, 108, 135 ... 270); four lanes or more over 75 s get a 30-second encore (waves, mirrored pairs, ripples; `Style.WAVE`) before the crescendo instead of a slower body, so the Ultimate's 90 s are 396 rockets (40 on an outside lane, 39 in the middle; 4.4 a second against the minute's 4.5). Peaks are the lane count before the finale and twice it in it; `capped` defers anything over `BODY_CAP` 10 / `FINALE_CAP` 20 a tick, which no shipped tier reaches. `forLaunchers(plan, lanes, n)` maps a lane to the nearest launcher by place (a lane exactly between two takes turns: 0.38.1's middle for 3 onto 2), and in a plan of 4+ lanes a rocket that finds all three heights taken on its launcher moves 2 ticks later, up to 8 times (the Ultimate on one launcher ends 2 ticks late, never a height twice). Ten colour sets (`COLOUR_SETS`, warm to cool, no colour in two; a lane takes the set at its place, so three lanes keep 0.38.1's `LANE_COLOURS`). **Holiday shows** (`arcade/HolidayShows`, pure; `arcade.holiday_shows { enabled, windows: [{ id, name, from, to, colours, fades, shape }] }`): Halloween 10-24..10-31, Christmas 12-18..12-26, New Year 12-31..01-01 (over New Year), Valentine's 02-10..02-14, St Patrick's 03-14..03-17 and Independence Day 07-01..07-05, local dates in `clock.time_zone`, the first listed winning an overlap; colours are hex or `DyeColor.getFireworkColor()` names; a window that can't be read is one WARN naming every problem and skipped; the section absent is `HolidayShows.SHIPPED`, the same six as literals (pinned to the bundled parse), and the backfill writes the section with its comment at every start, so Halloween is on from the first start. `themeOn(LocalDate)` is asked once when a long show starts (`FireworkShows.themeToday`): `MegaShow.look(shot, lanes, theme)` takes the theme's colours and fades (each lane starting on its own), its shape on one rocket in four (`signature`), every other finale rocket, and a gold in its first two colours; `FireworkShow.look(theme, kind, burst, index)` themes the Grand show's long bursts and finale through `Fireworks.spawn(at, power, look...)` (the one tagged spawn); a short burst and Party Time never. The start and server-wide lines name the tier and the holiday with the right article ("✦ <name> started an Ultimate Firework Show!", "... a Halloween Mega Firework Show!"); with no theme 0.38.1's lines word for word. The Prize Counter tiles of a themed row and My Show say "Halloween colours tonight!". **The Firework Remote** (`PrizeType.REMOTE`, appended; a glinting RECOVERY_COMPASS, token-prize tagged, never used up): `CustomBlockListener.onInteract` steps aside for it on a launcher right after the Mega check; `PrizeItemListener.remote` knows a launcher first (tag, else record), leaves another of ours and an interactable block (a door, a chest) to their own tap, and otherwise links or unlinks the launcher (`RemoteService.tap`) or opens My Show. `RemoteRules` (pure): a set of up to 10 in tap order (`game_prefs` `arcade.launcher.linked`, `"v1|x,y,z,world|..."`, deduplicated, capped, cleaned on read with one WARN; another version is read as empty and kept until the player changes it), `toggle` (link at the end, unlink and renumber, full at 10), ten number colours (`NUMBER_DYES`/`CODES`, for the holograms, the map flags, the My Show wool and the lore), eight-way directions, and `Taps` (a held tap is one: 600 ms, restarted by every repeat; My Show never opens within it). `RemoteService`: the cache, pruned against the records at load, join and use; `launcherGone` unlinks a broken launcher from every set (the online ones from the cache with their lore, holograms and map, the rest through one `PrizeDao.prefsNamed` read, the one new DAO read); the Remote's name and lore ("#3 · north-east, 45 blocks · x 120 y 70 z -340") refreshed on link, unlink, a held-slot change and a swap to it, and for every Remote carried, held or not, on a pickup, a world change and a Prize Counter hand-over; the launcher's own tap says "Linked as #2 in your show." (`LauncherRules.tapLine(..., linkedAs)`). `RemoteMarkers`: one `TextDisplay` per linked launcher, hidden from everyone and never saved inside its spawn's consumer and shown only to its viewer, while the Remote is in either hand, for launchers a show could fire from now (`MegaShowService.ticking`: loaded, asked first, and entity-ticking) within 96 blocks, renumbered in place, swept by `Keys.REMOTE_MARKER` (`EntitiesLoadEvent` and at start), down on not-held, quit, unload and disable. `MyShowMenu`: a Bedrock-safe chest screen, ten info-only wool tiles (stack count = the number; every fact in the name, `RemoteRules.tileName`: where, x/y/z, whose, and "not loaded" by the show's own test), Get my Show Map, Clear all (confirmed) and Close. **The Show Map** (`ShowMap` pure, `ShowMapRenderer`): a vanilla filled map at scale 2 (512 blocks, centred on the player, reused within 128 blocks, one a player: `game_prefs` `arcade.launcher.show_map`, `Keys.SHOW_MAP`, token-prize tagged, no cartography copies), a non-contextual renderer after vanilla's adding `MapCursor`s: linked launchers as banner flags in their numbers' colours captioned "#n" (pinned to the edge when off the map), the owner's other launchers as white X's, nobody else's, the map's own world only; it redraws only when the owner's version changes and never throws; renderers are re-attached at start and join. Geyser drops every caption but tints the banners and draws the X's as black crosses, so the colours tell them apart on Bedrock. No NMS. **Review fixes (R382):** every write of a set goes through `RemoteService.save`, which bumps its version (the sets of players who are away and a set pruned at a join redraw their Show Map too), a join redraws once, and `CustomBlockListener.staleLauncher` calls `launcherGone`; "Get my Show Map" takes the owner's other copies from the bag, the cursor, the ender chest and the ground (the last one dropped), and a Show Map is a plain item on the ground (`PrizeSafety.plainOnGround`: it despawns), so no pile builds up; a launcher tapped with nothing of ours in the main hand and a Remote in the off hand is the Remote's tap (`RemoteRules.offHandTap`, `RemoteService.tapFromOffHand`: a Java tap on a dropper never sends the off hand's use); a set that couldn't be read is read again only after 5 s, warning once in a row (`RemoteRules.Retries`), not on every 10-tick marker pass; and the docs say a place over the limit (not a "fourth"), "10 or more placed" for the rollback, and list the Remote and the launchers as kept for good. **Using a show:** with a linked set, `MegaShow.pickLinked` takes the first N usable in link order (another world, no record, not entity-ticking, air: counted, the gone ones unlinked), one line names what was skipped, none usable is refused with its reasons and kept, fewer fold; with no set, 0.38.1's nearest path. The games refusal, one long show, the busy guard (a linked launcher in a show refuses the whole show) and the 4-show cap stay. A linked set has no range beyond being entity-ticking (about 160 blocks from someone), but rockets are seen from about 32 blocks: README says to link near where you'll watch. **Limits:** `arcade.launcher.per_player` 15 (read 1-30); revision 24 moves 0.38.1's frozen 3 to 15 and keeps an owner's own value with one WARN. `ShippedComments` gains two revision-24 rows (0.38.1's Prize Counter header and `arcade.launcher` comment, fingerprints from `config-0.38.1.yml`, 36539d2's verbatim). **Back to 0.38.1:** the jar alone (one WARN, nothing runs); 0.38.1 reads `launchers` 4, 5 and 10 as 3, so the Super, Ultra and Ultimate rows sell as Mega shows at their own prices (README's switch-off line), `type: remote` is skipped with one WARN and a Remote does nothing, Show Maps are plain maps, `per_player: 15` reads as 10, the holiday section is ignored, and the linked sets come back after the upgrade. Tests: `MegaShowLanesTest`, `MegaShowLinkedTest`, `HolidayShowsTest`, `HolidayLookTest`, `RemoteRulesTest`, `ShowMapTest`, `RemoteRoutingTest`, `PrizeSafetyTest`, `FireworkRemoteMigrationTest`, and the 0.38.2 cases in `MegaShowPlanTest`, `MegaShowRulesTest`, `LauncherRulesTest`, `LauncherConfigTest`, `PrizeExtraParseTest`, `GivePrizeCommandTest`, `FireworksTest`, `ShippedCommentsTest`, `GrandShowMigrationTest`, `ShopMigrationTest`, `EventRowsMigrationTest` and `RollbackNotesTest`.
>
> **v17.24 changelog (0.38.1-firework-shows — the Grand and Mega Firework Shows, the Firework Launcher and a gentler Candy Apple shower):** The owner's requests of 25 Oct: the daughter "wanted to ask if we can make a little more expensive one that would be a bigger firework show (I think she means longer)", "You can tune back the candy apples. I have about 60 of them at this point and we got the bag.", and then "Make one more level at like 75 that is even bigger: 3 droppers that I have to set up, a full blown show with 3 firing locations", "maybe we do less at a time and spread out the show to like a minute long; it would be for those special events." **Config revision 23** (`GrandShowMigration`); no schema change (v38). **Firework rows gain `seconds`** (3-30, default 3, one WARN on a bad value; `PluginConfig.fireworkSeconds` into `PrizeExtra.seconds`), and a used item's length is its row's at use time (`TokenPrizes.prizeId` → the row; a row that is gone or is no longer a firework row plays 3: `FireworkShow.seconds(Prize)`). **The schedule is pure** (`arcade/FireworkShow.schedule`): a 3-second show is exactly the old one (6 bursts, 10 ticks apart, of 2 within 3 blocks at power 1); a longer one bursts every 10 ticks for its whole length, 3 a burst, 2.5-5.5 blocks out, alternately at power 0 and 1 (a mix of heights, more of it in a first-person view), then a finale of 9 at once, each in its own slice of the circle 3-5 blocks out at power 2, 20 ticks after the last burst (the 15-second show: 30 bursts and the finale, 99 fireworks, never more than 9 at once). `Fireworks.spawn(Location, int power)` sets the flight. **`FireworkShows`** plays each show on one timer: one show of 6 seconds or more a player at a time (`FireworkShows.Guard` numbers each show, and only a long show's own end frees its player); another long one is refused with "Wait for your show to finish!" (said at most once every 2 s a player, since a held Bedrock tap repeats its use) and not used up; short shows are never refused, stacked or not; a quit stops that player's shows (`PrizeItemListener.onQuit`) and disable cancels every timer. **The new row** `firework_grand` (COSMETICS, "&6Grand Firework Show", "A long firework show" / "with a big finale!", 40 tokens, FIREWORK_ROCKET, `seconds: 15`, no limit) ships right after `firework_show`; the Arcade Crate names its prizes, so it isn't in it. **Revision 23** inserts that row after `firework_show` once, only while no row has its id (at the end when the owner deleted `firework_show`; an owner's own `firework_grand` is kept with a WARN and the paste line), and moves `arcade.events.candy.every_minutes` 20 → 60, `last_day_every_minutes` 10 → 30 and `apples_per_shower` 6 → 3, each only from what 0.37.1-0.38.0 shipped (an owner's value is kept with a WARN naming the new default; an absent key is the backfill's); `CandyConfig.Candy.DEFAULTS` and the bundled section match, so a shower comes at ten past each hour (:10 and :40 on 31 Oct) and the shipped restart hold never skips one. Its tables are frozen literals. `ShippedComments` gains a revision-23 row for the Prize Counter's header (0.38.0's text, whose firework line said "three-second"), so a key is listed once a revision. `newerConfigWarning(revision, built)` states what an older gate says. **Back to 0.38.0:** the jar alone; 0.38.0 runs nothing on revision 23 (one WARN) and keeps the gentler candy (values in the file), but it doesn't know `seconds`, so it reads `firework_grand` as a plain three-second Firework Show at 40 tokens, with no one-at-a-time guard: README's "If you roll back to 0.38.0" sets `enabled: false` on it for a longer rollback, and an unused Grand show plays its 15 seconds again after the upgrade. **Review fixes:** a long show stays out of the games (`FireworkShows.playsHere`, on `Whereabouts`' facts, as `PartyService.guest`): each timer step, before it bursts, ends a long show whose player is in a game session or a Games world, and one used there is refused with "Save your show for after the games!" and kept; a long show's tap on one of ours (`PrizeItemListener.ours`: a HomeCraft block, a join sign, a Mini, a placed Arcade Trophy) returns before anything is cancelled or used up, as for a hat, since those blocks' own listeners run first; a long show's start stamps the refusal gap (`Guard.tryStart(player, isLong, now)`), so the held use that started it isn't refused aloud a moment later. A short show is untouched by all three. Revision 23's candy INFO line says only what the moved values mean (`Step.says`), and one more WARN names a last day that would now get fewer showers than an owner's own `every_minutes` under 30. README's `/hcm candy` example is a 7:10 PM shower, one the shipped schedule has. **The Firework Launcher** (`CustomBlockType.FIREWORK_LAUNCHER`, appended; a `placed_blocks` row like every custom block, owner = the placer): a dropper pointing up (`arcade.launcher { material: DROPPER, name, per_player: 3, range: 32 }`, `skins.firework_launcher` blank; `PluginConfig.Launcher`, `per_player` 1-10 and `range` 8-64 with one WARN each), built by `CustomItems.fireworkLauncher` with the `hcm:token_prize` tag (id `firework_launcher`, `MegaShow.LAUNCHER_PRIZE_ID`) on every one made, so it never becomes money and a place and a break can't wash the tag off, and with its placed state facing up (`BlockDataMeta`; `CustomBlockService.faceUp` again on place, before the record). Economy worlds only. A place counts the placer's records after the economy and town checks (`LauncherRules.counted`: never the spot being placed, nor a record whose block is air in a loaded chunk, which is forgotten as it is found), and a fourth is refused kindly with the item kept, admins too. `CustomBlockGuard.ownerOnly` adds it to the placer-or-admin breaks ("This is <name>'s Firework Launcher. Only they can take it down."); it holds no stock and drops one launcher. A tap says whose it is, "(2 of 3 set up)" and whether a show is using it (`LauncherRules.tapLine`) and never opens it; with a Mega show in the hand the tap is the show's. `onLauncherFeed` (`InventoryMoveItemEvent`, either side), `onLauncherDispense`, `onLauncherClick` (Paper's `BlockFailedDispenseEvent.shouldPlayEffect(false)`: no click, no smoke) and `onLauncherOpen` keep it a dropper in looks only, on the tile's own tag (`CustomBlockService.isLauncher`), never a database read; pistons, flows, explosions, withers and fire leave it standing as every placed HomeCraft block. **The rows** `firework_launcher` (COSMETICS, "&6Firework Launcher", "Set up 3 of them" / "for a Mega Firework Show!", 5 tokens, DROPPER, `type: block`, `block: firework_launcher`, no limit) and `firework_mega` (COSMETICS, "&dMega Firework Show", "A whole minute of fireworks" / "from your Firework Launchers!", 75 tokens, FIREWORK_ROCKET, `type: firework`, `seconds: 60`, `launchers: 3`, no limit) ship right after `firework_grand`, neither in the Arcade Crate; `PrizeExtra.launchers` (a seventh component; the six-argument constructor stays and gives 0) is read on a firework row, 0-3, and a launcher row's `seconds` are its own 45-90 (60 by default; `PluginConfig.megaSeconds`), so every other firework row keeps 3-30 and the 15- and 40-token shows are as they were; `launchers` on any other type is one WARN, ignored. `PrizeItems` gives the Mega item its own lore and a glint. **The Mega show's plan is pure** (`arcade/MegaShow`): a 60-second template in half-second beats over three lanes (left, middle and right as the user faced them at the start): an opening walk of single sparks left to right, four slow chases across and back, seven pairs, four volleys from all three, an 18-rocket crescendo whose gaps shrink as it climbs, a breath, and an 18-rocket triple finale (all three LOW, MID, HIGH, then low and high together, then gold from every launcher): 81 rockets, 1.35 a second, never more than 3 in a tick before the finale and 6 in it, the last burst at 59.8 s. A 45-90 s row stretches the body (`round(beat × 10 × (S × 20 − 100) / 1100)`) and keeps the finale's 100 ticks, so it always ends 4 ticks before its length. `forLaunchers` folds the same rockets onto 1 or 2 launchers (left on the first, right on the second, the middle's alternately), and a rocket that would go up from one launcher in the same tick at the same height as one before takes the nearest free height (`free`). Heights are flights to the burst (24, 30, 36 ticks: about 13, 20 and 28 blocks up); each lane has its own three colours (no colour in two lanes, kept with 1-2 launchers), and each part its own shape (no creeper face). `Fireworks.spawn(Location, colours, fade, type, flicker, trail, ticksToDetonate)` goes through the one tagged spawn, the look before the flight. **`MegaShowService`** plays it. Every refusal comes first, in `MegaShow.refusal`'s order, and keeps the item: in a game or a Games world ("Save your show for after the games!"), the player's long show on (0.38.1's guard: "Wait for your show to finish!"), no launcher near ("Set up a Firework Launcher nearby first!"), one of the chosen in a show (refused, never moved on to farther free ones), 4 playing ("The sky is full of shows!"). Then `FireworkShows.holdLong` takes the player's one long show (so a Grand show and a Mega show never play together for one player) and `Claims.tryClaim` the launchers (a claim that fails gives the long show back). The launchers are the nearest (anyone's) within `range` of the user in their world, at most the row's `launchers`, from one `findByType` read, in chunks that are loaded (asked first: none is ever loaded) and entity-ticking, an air record forgotten, ordered left to right along the user's right hand (`leftToRight`). One timer a show, every tick: it ends when its world is gone or none of its launchers' chunks is entity-ticking, fires each due rocket from its launcher's top (skipped while that chunk isn't ticking, or once the block isn't what it was), and ends at its last burst. A quit (`onQuit`) ends the user's shows and disable `stop()`s them all, before the database closes; walking away, a teleport or a death never ends one, and one cut short is not refunded (as Party Time). A launcher is in one show at a time (`Claims`, numbered: only a show's own end frees its launchers, red-team ECON-2's shape). One line to the user and one server-wide (`PrizeService.announce`, once a minute a player). `PrizeItemListener`'s FIREWORK case reads the row first and sends a Mega row to `megaShow` before 0.38.1's path: a tap on another of ours keeps it, a tap on a launcher, a block or the air starts it, a refusal keeps it (said at most once every 2 s), and only a show that started is used up. **Plan risk R3:** `PrizeItemListener.onPlace` stores a token-prize head as a placed prize only when it isn't a HomeCraft block item (`storesAsPlacedPrize`, as `MiniHeadListener` does), so a skinned launcher can't go to the bag and drop as well. **Revision 23** also adds `firework_launcher` and `firework_mega` after `firework_grand` (`GrandShowMigration.ADDED`, frozen: each after the one before it, or at the end without it; an id the owner uses is kept with a WARN and the paste line), with one INFO naming all it added, and the Prize Counter header it refreshes names `launchers` and the `firework_launcher` block. **Back to 0.38.0** with launchers placed: README says to pick up every launcher first (0.38.0 reads an unknown placed type as a retired Mini Workbench, which anyone with build rights there may break into a Mini Workbench item, no longer a prize), and to set `enabled: false` on `firework_mega` (a three-second show at 75 tokens there) as on `firework_grand`; 0.38.0 skips the launcher row with one WARN. Its short note no longer ends "Nothing is lost" (review LAUNCHER-ROLLBACK-4): until the upgrade, don't place a launcher (0.38.0 places the item as a plain dropper, which breaks into a plain dropper: that launcher is gone) and don't use a Grand or Mega show (spent as a three-second show); a launcher in the bag and an unused show are as they were after the upgrade. **Launcher review fixes:** `arcade.launcher.material` is a DROPPER or a DISPENSER (`PluginConfig.launcherMaterial`, `LAUNCHER_MATERIALS`): anything else, such as an OBSERVER or a TARGET, which has no tile and so no tag, is read as DROPPER with one WARN. `PrizeItemListener.megaShow` knows a launcher as `CustomBlockListener` does (`MegaShow.onLauncher`: the tile's tag, else the placed-block record), so a Mega show's tap on a launcher is never a tap nobody answers (review LAUNCHER-NONTILE-2, MEGA-RT-1, CFG-LAUNCHER-MATERIAL-1). A held tap on a launcher or a held fourth place (a held use repeats about five times a second, and a launcher opens no screen to stop it) says its line, and the fourth place its refused sound, at most once every 2 s a player for the same thing (`LauncherRules.Lines`, keyed by the launcher's spot or the refusal's text, so another launcher's tap is said at once); a tap line kept quiet reads no records, and a quit forgets them (`CustomBlockListener.onQuit`; review LAUNCHER-SPAM-3). Tests: `FireworkShowTest`, `GrandShowMigrationTest` (with `config-0.38.0.yml`, f62850f's verbatim), the firework cases in `PrizeExtraParseTest`, the revision-23 cases in `ShippedCommentsTest`, `ShopMigrationTest` and `EventRowsMigrationTest`, and the new defaults and README's example in `CandyConfigTest` and `CandyCommandTest`; for the Mega show `MegaShowPlanTest`, `MegaShowPlaceTest`, `MegaShowClaimsTest`, `MegaShowRulesTest`, `LauncherRulesTest`, `LauncherConfigTest`, and the launcher cases in `CustomBlockListenerTest`, `CustomBlockPistonTest`, `VendingBreakTest`, `PrizeExtraParseTest`, `PlacedPrizeTest`, `FireworksTest`, `ShippedCommentsTest`, `GrandShowMigrationTest` and `RollbackNotesTest`, and for the launcher review fixes the material, tap and line cases in `LauncherConfigTest`, `MegaShowRulesTest`, `LauncherRulesTest`, `CustomBlockListenerTest` and `RollbackNotesTest`.
>
> **v17.23 changelog (0.38.0-shop-and-trade — the token shop, worn-look rentals, the Keepers and the trade window):** SHOP-V2-SPEC §8 "0.38.0" (C0, S2, S3, S4, S5, S6, S7). **Config revision 22** (`ShopMigration`) and **schema v37 and v38**; every migration block now runs with its `setSchemaVersion` in one `Database.transaction` (D39), so a block lands whole or not at all. **The contract (C0):** `PrizeTab` gains LOOKS and KEEPERS (Prize Counter tabs in slots 1-8, the hub's in 19-26, Give a present at 47), `PrizeType` BALL and PARTY, `LimitPer.MONTH`; `Prize` gains `PrizeExtra` (`hatMaterial`, `renewTokens`, `ballLook`, `onlyIf`, `seconds`, `keeper`); `util/Whereabouts` is the one copy of "home from games / in a game / a Games world"; `PrizeService.bound(ItemStack)` (`hcm:bound_to`, or a `keeper_` id) is the one test the guard, the trade window and presents call. v37 = five columns on `cosmetics_owned` (`kind`, `since`, `notice_for`, `notice_stage`, `given_by`; every old row is a TRAIL, a HAT row's `enabled` is always 0, and the "any trail on" and `enableOnly` queries are scoped to TRAIL, so a 0.37 jar still reads the table right); v38 = the `trades` log. **The shop core (S2, §2, §5.9-5.11, §7):** revision 22 moves what still holds 0.35-0.37.3's value: the three trails to 30 days, the six animal hats to the Looks tab as 90-day rentals (a hat the owner only gave a picture keeps it), the Arcade Trophy to `costs: [750, 1000, 1250, 1500]` with a LIFETIME 99 limit, the crate's hat row out (its weight to fireworks, 15 → 18); 32 new rows are appended (Bouncy Boots, Floaty Feather, Make it Day, Sunny Day, five trails, Party Time, the Creeper and Dragon Hats, four caps, ten golf balls, the trophy ladder and the Trophy of the Month) whatever happened to the old rows; an edited row is kept with a WARN and its paste line; three bundled comments are refreshed by fingerprint. A startup INFO line names hats bought before 0.38 (grandfathered: permanent, placeable, tradeable). **Every limited buy is one transaction** (D30): the spend, `recordPurchase` (now throwing) and a trophy's serial commit together, and a trophy that doesn't fit the bag is queued to the Mailbox inside it, never dropped. Trophies are named from their rows ("Bronze Trophy #N", "Bought by"), with a broadcast at most once a minute a player; `only_if` (NIGHT, RAIN) refuses before any charge and is checked again after the commands, refunding a command that changed nothing (`execute as %uuid% at @s ...`, so the buyer's world); Party Time is one party at a time, two harmless fireworks a second around each economy-world player not in a game and not hiding others; hunt gear hides with no wild hunt; every trail row whose particle needs data is hidden with a WARN; golf balls are `'life'` claims with the `golf.ball = ball:<id>` pref, drawn on both editions. **The wardrobe (S3, §3, D2-D9, D13, D23, D40):** a worn look is a **right** in `cosmetics_owned`; a hat or cap item is a **key** bound to its owner (`hcm:bound_to`, `look_end`). Looks end at local midnight; buy again opens 7 days before the end and stays open 30 days after, at the same price (`renew_tokens` on a HAT row only); nothing renews by itself; a buy, a renewal or a present writes the right and takes the tokens in one transaction (a double confirm is charged once). Heads-up titles and chat lines 7 days and 1 day before and at the end, held in games and Games worlds, 100 ticks after a join; the giver of a present gets one line a week before. At the end the newest kept trail or a bagged collector cap comes on. `LookRules` (pure) holds the dates, the window, the price, `due` and each key's `verdict` (never delete on an unknown answer); `LookService` sweeps the bag, armour, off-hand, ender chest and every bundle and shulker inside them (one key per look per holder) at join, at a world change and every minute; `BoundItemGuard` (decisions in `GuardRules`) keeps a key out of every container but the owner's own ender chest and off frames, stands, pots, shelves and allays, deletes one left in a block's or entity's container on close, never touches a player's own inventory under `/invsee` or `/ec`, and never refuses a Creative inventory; furnaces don't smelt prizes. **My Looks** (54) and the look screen (27): one tap wears a hat or switches a trail; collector caps listed with where they are. Presents now carry rentals and golf balls (never a Keeper). `/hcm account move` moves every economy record of one UUID to another in one transaction (Floodgate linking is a new account). **The Keepers (S4, §5.1-5.6, §5.8, D12):** seven rows in the jar (`arcade/Keepers.java`, ids `keeper_*`, `SHIPPED_IDS` append-only; a config row with that prefix is skipped), merged Keepers, then Collectors, then config rows: the Legend Crown 5,000 (with the Royal Trail and its number in `arcade_state`), the Aurora, Stardust and Frost trails 3,000, the Wizard Hat and the Knight's Helm 2,500; one each, kept for good, never a present, never traded; `buyKeeper` is one transaction. Each look and pattern ships in two forms, picked by `arcade.keepers.looks.<id>` at enable (a sweep rebuilds an owned key of the other form in place), and each worn Keeper shows a **mark** (one puff of its reserved particle every 2 s while walking; `arcade.keepers.marks`). `KeeperSelfTest` builds both forms and checks every particle; the Crown loses its Java-only EQUIPPABLE / ITEM_MODEL with one WARN if they don't take. `TrailService` runs patterns and sends each puff per viewer (`showsHere`, `sees`: 32 blocks, `canSee`, never in a game, a Games world, spectator or invisible), and a viewer may hide other people's trails, marks and parties (`game_prefs` `arcade.trails.hide_others`, My Looks slot 46, `/hcm trail hide|show`). A placed Arcade Trophy opens the Arcade on the machine's own lines in `CustomBlockListener` (sneak passes, `hcm.arcade.use`, a Bedrock hat tap doesn't wear the hat; `arcade/ArcadeTrophies`). **The trade window (S5, §4, D21, D36, D37, D41):** `/trade`, the crouch gesture (an empty hand), the PC's Trade site; a 54-slot window per player over one `TradeSession` (OPEN → COUNTDOWN → SWAPPING → DONE / CANCELLED), ghosts on the table (every plugin key stripped, containers built empty), the change lock, the give-away second tap, a 3 s countdown, and a one-tick swap in a pinned order (`TradeSwap`: verify, plan, take the real stacks, money out, one transaction writing the PENDING row, HELD Mailbox rows, Mini owners and collector claims, give, mark both bags, money in, save, then DONE and READY). Escrow is by reference: a close, quit, death, teleport, world or game-mode change or a stop just cancels. `ItemFacts` reads every nested item fail closed; `TradeRules` holds the refusal table (game items, Keepers, rented looks, packages, display copies, the Trick-or-Treat Bag, `refuse_items`, collector claims) and T3. Minis move owner with a price-0 sale row (venue TRADE or GIFT); `MiniDao.lastSalePrice` counts only priced sales. `/hcm trades`, `show` and `settle release|void` (a PENDING trade WARNs at enable and waits). `hcm.trade.use`, the `trade:` section. **The faucet side (S6, §5.12):** the login streak and playtime are paid in a Games world the Games allow (still once a day; the achievement sweep stays in economy worlds); `games.everyday_cap` (a third cap over every DAILY_CHALLENGE reward, per player per local day) ships 0 = off. **Integration (S7):** a Keeper key dragged onto the head starts its mark at once; the hide switch is read for online players at enable; the Bedrock "Trade?" screen closes with its request. **Back to 0.37.3:** the jar alone; README "If you roll back to 0.37.3" lists the quirks (an unlimited Monthly, Make it Day at noon, every trophy bought then named "Arcade Trophy #N" for good, rental keys as plain prizes, unguarded Keepers, held trade overflow) and the eight `enabled: false` lines (the five ladder trophies, the Monthly, Make it Day, Sunny Day). Tests: `PrizeExtraParseTest`, `LookDaoTest`, `DatabaseMigrationTest`, `PrizeDaoScopeTest`, `BoundKeyTest`, `WhereaboutsTest`, `PrizeTabSlotsTest`, `ShopMigrationTest`, `PrizeShelfTest`, `TrophyNameTest`, `ArcadeTrophyPriceTest`, `PartyTest`, `OnlyIfTest`, `LimitTxTest`, `LegacyHatsLineTest`, `GolfBallTest`, `LookRulesTest`, `LookPurchaseTxTest`, `GuardRulesTest`, `BoundItemGuardTest`, `WearTest`, `StoredAtTest`, `LookSweepTest`, `NoticeHoldTest`, `NoticeGateTest`, `MyLooksLayoutTest`, `GiftLooksTest`, `LooksCommandTest`, `AccountMoveTest`, `MyLooksTapTest`, `EndFallbackTest`, `BuyFeedbackTest`, `KeepersTest`, `KeeperVariantTest`, `KeeperRulesTest`, `KeeperMarkTest`, `TrailPatternTest`, `TrailViewersTest`, `ArcadeTrophiesTest`, `PartyHideTest`, `TradeRulesTest`, `TradeSessionTest`, `TradeRoomTest`, `TradeVerifyTest`, `TradeRequestsTest`, `TradeSwapTxTest`, `GiveAwayTest`, `BedrockOfferTest`, `GameModeCancelTest`, `GhostTest`, `TradeDaoTest`, `MiniPriceHistoryTest`, `TradeMenuLayoutTest`, `GestureTest`, `TradeBoundTest`, `StreakInGamesWorldTest`, `EverydayCapTest`, `MarkOnDragTest`, `RollbackNotesTest`, `TradeSyncTest`, `SaveCheckTest`. **The red-team fixes:** a free `/hcm give prize` of a command, party or extra-home row goes through `deliverAsOne` like a bought one (`asOne`, `handedOverAsOne`; it used to hand a null item on); `only_if: night` reads the world's clock (`dayOnClock`, 26.3's dark hours 12523-23477), never `World#isDayTime`, whose sky value is worked out again only on the next tick, so Make it Day was refunded every time, and a fixed-time world refuses before any charge; a party is on until its own timer ends it (`PartyService.Schedule` numbers each party, and a timer cancels only itself), so an old timer can't cut a later party short; a trade's yes waits on the request (`TradeRequests.sayYes`) until neither player has a container open or a stack on the cursor (`TradeRules.windowOpens`), then both windows open; a give-away's second tap counts only `GIVE_AWAY_SETTLE_MS` (600) after the first; a present's moment leaves a trade window and the "Trade?" screen open; the swap marks each bag with the trade's id as it hands the stacks over (`hcm:trade_seen`, in the player's own saved data, so the mark reaches the disk with the bag), and the next start reads each PENDING trade's marks (`OfflinePlayer.getPersistentDataContainer`) and writes where its bags are on the row (`TradeStore.Saved`: both, neither, one, or unread; a later start reads it again only exactly, so a later trade raising a mark can't change it): the WARN and `show` name the one verb that fits, a settle that doesn't fit is refused, and a trade only one bag was saved with is voided for that player, whose own offer goes READY to their Mailbox from the trade row (this review first rolled that bag back on its own at the player's join; the fourth review made it the admin's, below); money is never paid back by a settle; a bag save that didn't reach the disk leaves the trade PENDING, a DONE trade whose bag came back from before it is PENDING again at the next start with its uncollected rows HELD (the fifth review removed that, below), and the swap's Mailbox line counts only rows really written and says "will reach your Mailbox once the trade is checked" while they are held; the 10-tick sync's lowered stack says "<n> on the table now", not "off the table"; an offline giver's week-before line waits in `game_prefs` (`arcade.looks.giver_lines`); a bundle or shulker box holding a key is guarded as the key (`GuardRules.keysInside`), and a close or a spawn takes the keys out of it and keeps the rest; `/hcm looks give` says where the key went (`LookRules.givenLine`); Wear it takes a collector cap from the bag, then the off-hand (`LookRules.capSlot`); My Looks' slot 2 names a helmet that isn't her key. **The second review's fixes:** an end whose run start isn't known (`since` 0, a row from before v37: 0.37's crate and shop wrote the same rows) is quiet like a crate's day (`LookRules.quietEnd`: one chat line, no title, no price, no [Buy again]), and the start that brings v37 marks every 0.37 end already past as said (`LookDao.endsBeforeUpgrade`), so the first 0.38 join announces nothing 0.37 already had; several heads-ups at once title only those with a title of their own (`LookRules.titleFor`), so crate days ending together bring none up; a heads-up waits while a Bedrock player has any chest screen open (`LookRules.screenFree`: an Arcade cabinet is a chest screen with no game session, and the title went off unseen behind it); the join's 101-tick task ends its own join's wait (`NoticeGate.joinWaitOver`, a later join's stamp stays), so a catch-up after a slow tick no longer delays the heads-up a minute; the trade request's links run `/homecraftmanagement:trade accept|deny` and the hints name `/hcm trade` where another plugin owns `/trade` (`TradeRules.click`, `typed`); `/hcm trades <name>` reads the server's own records only (`LooksCommand.offline`; no web lookup on the main thread, and an unknown name is said so); `/hcm looks give` refuses `[days]` on a Keeper and its line says "for good", is no present from the console (`LookRules.givenAsPresent`: a plain line, no "CONSOLE gave you"), and `renew` / `gift` answer the console; the account move's dry run says to trade the items after the move (a Mini traded first makes the move refuse, and that refusal says how out); `/hcm give prize ball_*` says a ball is an unlock that Give a present gives; Bedrock's How book points at the table. **The third review's fixes:** an offline player the server has no saved data for (a missing or unreadable file, a world folder that isn't there) can't be read (`TradeStore.mark`), so a start against the wrong folder no longer reopens trades that finished, and each later start reads a PENDING row's bags again, exactly (only the trade's own mark holds it), so a definite answer that differs replaces an earlier one with a line in the WARN (TCR-FA-1); `Player.saveData` never throws (vanilla catches and logs a failed write), so the live save compares the player's file before and after (`SaveCheck`; `<level>/players/data`, else `playerdata`, else one WARN and no check) and a save that didn't reach the disk throws: the trade stays PENDING with `bag a|b unsaved` / `bags unsaved` on the row, which refuses both verbs (the live mark holds it either way) until the next start reads the disk (TCR-FA-3); the mark carries the trade's time (`hcm:trade_seen_at`), so the ids a restored database hands out again never match another trade's mark: its own id holds a trade only with its own time, a higher one only as a trade of this database with that player at that time (TCR-FA-4); a late restart still checks the trades just before the stop (RECHECK-WINDOW; the fourth review reads each DONE trade once instead, below); v38's `trades.moved` keeps one line per Mini owner and collector claim the swap moved (`MiniService.tradeOwners` returns the recorded owners), and a void puts each back in its own transaction only while it is as the swap left it (`MiniService.tradeBack`: the owner, and the trade's price-0 sale row; `unclaim` the receiver, then `claimOnce` the giver), naming any that moved on (D4); a deposit refunded at step 7 is 0 on the row (`TradeDao.paidBack`), so the WARN and the settle name only money that moved (MONEY-REFUND); the join's and a world change's look tasks act only while that join's connection is open (`isConnected`; Paper answers `isOnline` by UUID, so a first join's task took the giver's waiting lines to a closed connection after a quick rejoin, TT1-STALE-REJOIN); a present's giver also hears with the receiver's 1-day notice or the end when the week's was skipped, once a run (`LookRules.giverHears`, F1-GIVER-SKIPPED); the games guide states 0.37.3's fair-play rule (MERGE-1). **The fourth review's fixes:** a trade the server didn't see finish is only reported (TCR4-WORTH-1, TCR4-RESET-2): the automatic roll-back is gone (`TradeRollback`, `hcm:trade_undone`, the join and world-change listeners), since the crash it covered is a few milliseconds a trade while its false reads (a reset player file, one put back from a backup, a save that kept failing) took items from honest bags with no admin step and no way to stop it, and it waited on a join, a game or a player who never came back (R4FA-5, R4FA-6). The next server start writes where the bags are on the row with one WARN, and the admin settles: `/hcm trades settle <id> release|void [player] [confirm]`, where `void <player>` says only that player's bag holds the trade (the admin takes the other side's offer out of it first) and sends their own offer READY to their Mailbox from the row, online or not (TCR4-2), and `confirm` goes against the read and says so on the row ("confirmed over ..."); a void is never allowed over two bags that hold the trade, and nothing is while a bag save that didn't reach the disk waits for a server start (TCR4-OVERRIDE-3). `show` reads each bag now, exactly, and says what each verb does. A bag with no mark carries its player file's time (`TradeStore.Mark.saved`): a file written after the trade swapped (a reset, a fresh world) or live data can't say, so it never reads as a bag from before every trade (R4FA-1); each DONE trade was read once, at the first server start after it (v38 `trades.checked`; no window, so `TradeDao.lastDone` and `RECHECK_MS` are gone; the fifth review removed that read, below), and the start checks before it prunes (TCR4-6, NT-2); a recorded answer that one bag now reads against while the other can't say becomes unread, so either verb is open (R4FA-3); a plugin reload, told from a server start by a JVM-wide system property (`homecraft.trades.bootChecked`), reads and writes nothing (R4FA-4). A void leaves a Mini or a claim a later standing trade carried (a cap going home now writes a `cap` line on `moved`; `TradeDao.movedAfter`) and a Mini whose newest sale isn't the trade's own (`MiniDao.latestSale` in `MiniService.tradeBack`), and names each (R4FA-2, TCR4-1). Both sides' stacks are kept whole until the trade is settled (or, since the fifth review, finishes), then trimmed past 64 KB (`TradeDao.trim`, TCR4-5). v38's `a_before` and `b_before` keep each side's balance just before the swap's money moved, and the WARN, `show` and a settle name it beside the balance now (TCR4-3). The players' lines name only money that really moved (`TradeSwap.Result.paid`, `stuck`), and a payer whose money came back, or couldn't, is told (`TradeService.moneyBackLine`, NT-1). **The fifth review's fixes:** no start reads a finished trade (AW-1, AW-2): a trade goes DONE only once both player files changed after the swap, so a bag that read from before it at the next start was a file or folder the admin put back, and the start's re-hold (DONE back to PENDING, its uncollected READY rows HELD again) was the last automatic Mailbox change, a false alarm whose advice ("what <a> gave is in both") destroyed what a backup taken before the offer was gained never held; and a first read that couldn't say left the trade to be read at every start, so a file put back weeks later reopened it. The read, `TradeDao.unchecked`/`checked`, `DeliveryDao.hold` and v38's `checked` column are gone (a test build's database keeps the column, unread); a finished trade is trimmed at once, and `show` reads each bag now for a DONE trade too, so an admin who put a file back looks at the trades since and sets right by hand only what they want undone. A void waits while a later trade that still waits moved one of its Minis or claims (`TradeStore.waitingAfter`: "settle #N first", `confirm` or not), since voiding the older of two such trades first left the owner and the claim with the wrong player (AW-3, SETTLE-ORDER-1); settled newest first, each void puts back what it moved (`TradeDao.movedAfter` now says which later trades wait). The prune keeps every row after the oldest PENDING one, so an old void still sees that a redo carried a claim, which has no sale row to say so (SETTLE-PRUNE-3). One bag alone read as saved after the players played on (a bag save that didn't reach the disk while both kept playing, or a saved bag whose mark is a later trade's) is written "bags unread, played on" (`TradeStore.PLAYED_ON`, read as unread and never read again) and names no verb, since `void <player>` hands that player's offer out again from the log and is exact only when nobody played after the swap (SETTLE-REHELD-COPY-2). `show` names what each Mailbox row holds ("#41 HELD for <a>: 40 Oak Log"), and the one-bag advice says to take the other's offer out less what `show` lists as held, so the admin removes exactly what reached the bag (SETTLE-HELD-AMOUNTS-4); the unread advice, which "played on" also gives, says the same for `void <player>` (R5-PLAYEDON-HELD-1).
>
> **v17.22 changelog (0.37.3-christmas — the Christmas collectors and the break fix):** SHOP-V2-SPEC §8 "0.37.3" (S0, S1, S1b), one release ahead of the token shop. No config revision (`CONFIG_REVISION` stays 21) and no schema change (v36), so the way back to 0.37.2 is the jar alone. **The Christmas collectors** (S0, §5.7): the **Santa Cap 2026** (`cap_santa_2026`, red leather with the rib trim in quartz, `CapLook(0xB02E26, "rib", "quartz")`, 250 tokens, 1-31 Dec 2026) and the **Twinkle 2026** trail (`trail_twinkle_2026`, `ELECTRIC_SPARK` at the feet, no data, 400, 1 Dec 2026-6 Jan 2027; `inSeason` compares whole dates, so a 2026 collector sells into January), appended to `Collectors.ROWS` and `SHIPPED_IDS`: giftable, LIFETIME 1, never from a crate, on the Cosmetics tab (0.38's C0 moves the caps to the Looks tab). `Collector` gains `season`, worked out from the start month (Oct Halloween, Dec Christmas, Apr/May Spring, Jul/Aug Summer, any other month Holiday) by the 5-argument constructor, which stays, so its callers compile unchanged. `Collectors.collectorLine` is the line on the cap and on both tiles, `<colour><season> <year> collector` (`seasonColour`: Halloween &6, Christmas &c, Spring &d, Summer &e), so Christmas reads red and Halloween stays gold. "Give a present" reads "Buy a collector / for someone who's online." `TRAIL_NOT_HERE` and the self-test's unknown-particle WARN read "That trail can't show on this server yet."; its no-trim WARN says "without its trim" (the Santa Cap's band is not vines). Every collector row is written through `Collectors.cap(...)` / `trail(...)`, Halloween's to the same values (pinned field by field against 0.37.1). **The break fix** (S1, §4.15, D17, D22; the owner's "a fix to include"): anyone with build rights could break a Vending Machine or Display Case and get every Mini in it, with overflow on the ground and no owner update; Pallets the same; all of it ran at HIGH before the break was final, and pistons, water and lava had no guard for our blocks or for a placed prize head. **Decide at HIGH, act at MONITOR:** `CustomBlockListener.onBreak` only decides (the placer or `hcm.admin`, before the unchanged town check: "This is <owner>'s <block>. Only they can take it down."); `onBreakFinal` (MONITOR, `ignoreCancelled`) does the rest in `block/CustomBlockGuard`'s order (the stock, the record, the muffler, the shop display, the block's own item), so a later cancel changes nothing, and a stock write that fails calls the break off with everything still in the block. **The stock goes home:** `VendingService.returnStock` and `PalletService.returnStock` delete the rows and queue READY Mailbox rows (`DeliveryService.queueReady`, which joins the caller's transaction) in one `Database.transaction`, each item to whoever stocked it (a Pallet: one row per full stack), never the breaker, never the ground; online owners are told, and so is an admin who breaks someone else's. **Pistons, flows, explosions and a wither** (LOW, `ignoreCancelled`) can't move or break a placed custom block, a Vending top or a placed prize head (`pistonRefused`, `flowRefused`; `changeRefused` for `EntityChangeBlockEvent`, which a hurt wither fires for every block it chews through, and for `BlockBurnEvent`, for a block set to a material that burns; a flow or change of air or liquid does no lookup). **A placed Mini head** follows the same order: `MiniHeadListener.onBreak` (HIGH) only decides (`trade/PlacedMiniRules`: a wild one is caught, a player who may not take it is refused), and `onBreakFinal` (MONITOR, `ignoreCancelled`) drops the exact copy and forgets the head, so a break cancelled later no longer leaves the head standing with a second copy on the ground. A wither that chews through a placed Mini head (`onEntityChangeBlock`) is cancelled and the exact copy drops, as for a flow, so it no longer leaves a plain head and an orphaned `placed_minis` row. **Placed prizes** (`arcade/PlacedPrizes`, `PlacedPrizeRules`) carry `trophy_placer` and `placed_prize_id`; only the placer or an admin breaks one ("That trophy belongs to <name>."), and the prize goes into the breaker's bag, else their Mailbox (source `PRIZE`); `PrizeItemListener`'s piston, flow, explosion and `dropAll` handlers are gone. A head placed before 0.37.3 has no placer and still breaks for anyone. **Write first, then give** (§11 #4, `util/GiveAfterWrite`): a Display Case reclaim, a Vending unlist, a Pallet take-back and a Mailbox collect write the delete, status or remainder, plus Mailbox rows for what won't fit, in one transaction before anything is given; a failed write gives nothing ("Could not save - try again."); `giveOrDrop` and `dropTo` are gone, and a bought Mini that doesn't fit goes to the buyer's Mailbox. **`carries` fails closed** (D36, §11 #9): a container whose contents can't be read, or that holds anything deeper than `TokenPrizes.MAX_DEPTH` (4), counts as holding a prize, so a prize six bundles deep no longer goes onto a Pallet; `TokenPrizes.contents(item, depth)` is the shared reader 0.38's trade window builds on. **`/hcm games check`** warns when two economy worlds are in different Multiverse-Inventories groups, since the Mailbox would carry things between them. **A newer file says so** (S1b, §7.9): a config.yml whose `config_revision` is past `CONFIG_REVISION`, or a database whose `schema_version` is past the last migration (what a rollback from 0.38 leaves), logs one WARN each at startup ("This config was written by a newer HomeCraft (revision 22); rows this version doesn't know are skipped."); nothing runs and nothing is lowered (`HomeCraftManagement.newerConfigWarning`, `Database.newerSchemaWarning`). **The live-test fixes** (the owner's tests on 19 and 22 Oct, both still happening after 0.37.1): **a Fresh Courses parkour target counts only over its pad** (`Geometry.firstHitOver`, `Geometry.standsOn`, `Progress.PAD_HAIR`, `ParkourPlanner.CHECKPOINT_PAD` and `FINISH_PAD`; see Time trials): a jump that fell short beside a checkpoint, one that slid down its side or was stopped flat against it, or feet on the edge of the pad before no longer count it (nor the finish), so a fall no longer sends the run back onto the pad it missed, and `ParkourValidator` refuses a mark off its pad's middle. **The fair-play watch judges values, not names** (`FairPlay.boost`; see Time trials): races voided both racers, the owner on Java and the daughter on Bedrock, around the switch from walking to running, and the bracket's text isn't in yet. 0.37.1's watch voided on any modifier key but `minecraft:sprinting` and `minecraft:powder_snow` and on any base off the player's own, in either direction, so a 0 amount, a slow-down or a re-keyed sprint from outside HCM voided like a real boost. Every source checked (vanilla 26.1 to 26.3, Paper, Geyser and Floodgate, Multiverse-Inventories, HCM itself) adds nothing else in honest play, so the culprit is another plugin, a datapack or a command block; now a run is voided only by a real boost (a speed past an honest sprint's 0.13, a higher jump, step or safe fall, any change of gravity, or a base past the player's own), and any other modifier is noted on the console once a key, so the next live test names it either way. **Back to 0.37.2:** the jar alone. The Christmas tiles go and owned Twinkle trails stop drawing until the re-upgrade (their rows stay); Santa Caps stay in bags; the break fix reverts, and so do the checkpoint fix and the value-judging watch; Mailbox rows already written stay and can be collected. Tests: `CollectorsTest`, `CollectorSeasonTest`, `CollectorSelfTestRulesTest`, `PrizeCounterCollectorTest`, `GiftPickerTest`, `CollectorMergeTest`, `VendingBreakTest`, `CustomBlockPistonTest`, `PlacedPrizeTest`, `PlacedMiniBreakTest`, `GiveAfterWriteTest`, `CarriesTest`, `DeliveryQueueTest`, `GamesCheckTest`, the newer-file cases in `ConfigMigrationTest` and `DatabaseMigrationTest`, the pad-column cases in `GeometryTest`, `ProgressTest`, `ParkourPlannerTest` and `ParkourValidatorTest`, and the value cases in `FairPlayTest`.
>
> **v17.21 changelog (0.37.1-mountain-halloween — the Mountain Run, wider and downhill; Halloween 2026, the collectors and the Candy Apple shower):** Three parts, one release. **The Mountain Run, wider and downhill** (boat planner ALGO 5, MOUNTAIN-V21-SPEC; the deck fix in DECK-DECISION). The owner's ride on 0.37.0 (Medium, 7 wide, 3:16, on Java) was "really cool" with "really great atmosphere" and "the length felt really great", but "felt very bad because the track is just so skinny... You need large spaces to manoeuvre", "no less than twice as wide as it is now it would be really fun!", and "it wasn't really downhill": 35 blocks in 23 drops, 13 of its 23 lips on the approach straights into the links and each row flat for 250-400 blocks; the wall arrows pointed "the wrong direction" and the forest's tree tops looked "cut off"; the 13-wide Slalom's width was "very nice". The owner asked for 18 / 15 / 14, about three times the descent, the length and the atmosphere kept. **The widths, no necks** (D1, D2): the Winding Road is 18 / 15 / 14 on Easy / Medium / Hard (the Slalom keeps 15 / 13 / 11) and keeps that width from the pit to the finish. 0.37.0 cut every 2-block drop on a lane wider than 7 to a 7-wide neck, which at these widths would have been 13-17 necks a run, 15-17% of it at 7 wide, exactly where it matters; so the leg rule changes, not the lane: a leg across a Big Drop may be **66** (`MountainValidator.MAX_LEG_BIG_DROP`; its least is the set-back c = W / 2 + 0.75 plus Z(2) 51 + 3 + 0.25, 60.5-64 on lanes of 11-18 and 65.5 with the raster's GROW), every other leg keeps 60, a Big Drop and the checkpoint before it stand only on an axis-aligned straight (off the axis an 18 lane would need 67), and the planner budgets GROW at the Final Drop and wherever one checkpoint serves two Big Drops. 60 was a comfort rule (Course Variety §2.6's "a reset never sends a boat far back") that nothing reads at runtime; the cost is a reset up to about 66 back after a Big Drop, a default the owner may still overrule (13-cell drop gates would cost about 3 Big Drops a Medium run). A forest no longer widens the lane (`widen(FOREST)` 0, it was 4 a side); a split and a snow drift widen by 2 a side, each way past them at least 7-7.5 wide. `RasterV4.narrowAt` is the width invariant. **The descent** (D3, D4): every drop may be a 2-block drop on every tier and style, so Easy has Big Drops and the Final Drop is planned as one (`DropPlan.Planner.finalDrop` falls back to a 1-block one where a Big one has no window or fails `finalOk`; it was 2 blocks on all 90 plans probed); the road deals 18-28 / 24-38 / 28-40 drops aimed at 32-44 / 48-64 / 52-68 blocks (floors 30 / 46 / 48), the Slalom 8-14 / 12-20 / 14-22 (floors 18 / 26 / 30). The drops spread through the rows: fill lays a lip on any straight (the "brake-worthy or ≥ 100" filter goes, and F-F's long straight is 60, not 100), a road lip's run-up is max(8, ⌈c⌉ + 2), 12 / 11 / 10 (the run-up the owner rode at 7 wide), the Slalom keeps 0.37.0's, and the reach bounds keep W + 3 (a piece and a gate field still leave the checkpoint before a lip its room). **Why not 3x** (D3, D15): descent comes only from drops, one checkpoint must stand between two lips and no lip in another's flight zone, so at most 2 blocks fall per ~63 blocks of straight (Z(2) 51 + LIP_GAP 12), and 0.37.0's straights were already full: a literal 3x Medium (108) exceeds even an all-straight track at this length (~87). It needs a road about twice as long (5-6 minutes, which breaks "the length kept") or a packed-ice fact that shrinks Z(2) from 51 to about 28-31; the flight recorder (D17) gathers that fact from the owner's own rides, for 0.38. **The windows** (D5): road T_m 100-130 s on Easy and 120-150 s on Medium and Hard (was 90-120 / 105-135), the Slalom's unchanged; a wide road is ridden faster (the owner rode the 7-wide road at 0.60 of model speed, the 13-wide Slalom at about 0.83-0.9), so at 0.75-0.85 of model speed a Medium road of T_m ~138 s should ride in about 2:40-3:05 (2:33-2:46 at the owner's Slalom rate); if it rides over 3:30, 0.37.2 lowers Medium and Hard to 110-140 (ALGO 6). **Snow** (D8, D9): plain and packed ice are both 0.98 in Java (and plain ice melts by the lanterns), blue is 0.989, snow and smooth sandstone both 0.6, so snow can't slow the track "a bit"; it replaces the sandstone in every slow cell (the tight bends' run-offs, the pits, now "snow drifts", and the paddock: `Palette.SNOW`, while the raster's class stays `SAND`, so no planner rule changes), with SNOWY BEND and SNOW DRIFT! signs, and the v5 proof takes snow that continues the track at its own level as a drive cell and slow ground (the snow flood; all other snow stays wall or scenery). **Blue** (D8): Medium's boost strips are 0-1, on straights only and at least 40 before an arc (9 of 0.37.0's 15 sat on bends, worth 0.0-0.1 s); Hard's blue straights are 1-2, each ending in a 1-block brake drop (the R ≥ 150 sweeper ending goes). **Arrows** (D7): `RasterV4.wallArrow` (facing = the way of travel turned anticlockwise) on a wall block whose one open face onto the lane shows the arrow flat, with a wood cap where glass stood over it: 5,429 of 5,429 faces point the way in 32 proven plans (0.37.0: 5 of 2,524). **Crowns** (D10): round crowns on every forest trunk (logs to ice + 8), wall trees in both walls, a 58-leaf island ball, every leaf through `canopy()`. **The view** (D6): `MountainScenery.BANK_HIGH` 5 → 2; step V measured the stand at 20-23% of the track on three Medium plans (22-25% at bank 1, 24-28% at 0), against 11-14% on 0.37.0's full-forest plans (12.2% on the owner's seed; one plan with 52 trees saw 23%), so 2 was kept (the spec tries 1 and 0 only when 2 doesn't beat 0.37.0's 14%); the start still sees none of the lower half (that needs a new summit). **Budgets** (D11): `MountainScenery.BUDGET` 280k → 290k, with a tree reserve of 20,500 (320 trees x 62.8 + 400) taken off the skin first, the far skin cut 6 columns at a time, and MountainSoakTest's p99 line 390k. **ALGO 5, not in place** (D12, D13): 0.37.0 is public, so an algo-4 layout may exist on some server;
